					<div id="mw-content-text" class="mw-body-content mw-content-ltr" lang="en" dir="ltr"><div class="mw-parser-output"><div class="shortdescription nomobile noexcerpt noprint searchaux" style="display:none">Spanish Catholic saint and missionary (1506–1552)</div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">This article is about the saint. For other uses, see <a href="/wiki/St._Francis_Xavier_(disambiguation)" class="mw-disambig" title="St. Francis Xavier (disambiguation)">St. Francis Xavier (disambiguation)</a>.</div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">"François Xavier" redirects here. For other uses, see <a href="/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois-Xavier" title="François-Xavier">François-Xavier</a> and <a href="/wiki/St._Fran%C3%A7ois_Xavier,_Manitoba" title="St. François Xavier, Manitoba">St. François Xavier, Manitoba</a>.</div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">In this <a href="/wiki/Spanish_name" class="mw-redirect" title="Spanish name">Spanish name</a>, the first or paternal&#32;<a href="/wiki/Surname" title="Surname">surname</a> is <i> Jasso</i>&#32;and the second or maternal family name is <i> Azpilicueta</i>.</div>

<p><b>Francis Xavier</b> (born <b>Francisco de Jasso y Azpilicueta</b>; <a href="/wiki/Latin_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Latin language">Latin</a>: <i>Franciscus Xaverius</i>; <a href="/wiki/Basque_language" title="Basque language">Basque</a>: <i>Frantzisko Xabierkoa</i>; <a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a>: <i>François Xavier</i>; <a href="/wiki/Spanish_language" title="Spanish language">Spanish</a>: <i>Francisco Javier</i>; <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_language" title="Portuguese language">Portuguese</a>: <i>Francisco Xavier</i>; 7 April 1506&#160;&#8211;&#32;3 December 1552), venerated as <b>Saint Francis Xavier</b>, was a <a href="/wiki/Navarre" title="Navarre">Navarrese</a> <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic</a> <a href="/wiki/Missionary" title="Missionary">missionary</a> and <a href="/wiki/Saint" title="Saint">saint</a> who was a co-founder of the <a href="/wiki/Society_of_Jesus" class="mw-redirect" title="Society of Jesus">Society of Jesus</a>.
</p><p>Born in <a href="/wiki/Javier,_Spain" class="mw-redirect" title="Javier, Spain">Javier</a> (Xavier in <a href="/wiki/Old_Spanish_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Old Spanish language">Old Spanish</a> and in <a href="/wiki/Navarro-Aragonese" title="Navarro-Aragonese">Navarro-Aragonese</a>, or Xabier, a <a href="/wiki/Basque_language" title="Basque language">Basque</a> word meaning "new house"), in the Kingdom of Navarre (in present-day <a href="/wiki/Spain" title="Spain">Spain</a>), he was a companion of <a href="/wiki/Ignatius_of_Loyola" title="Ignatius of Loyola">Ignatius of Loyola</a> and one of the first seven <a href="/wiki/Jesuits" title="Jesuits">Jesuits</a> who took vows of poverty and chastity at <a href="/wiki/Montmartre" title="Montmartre">Montmartre</a>, <a href="/wiki/Paris" title="Paris">Paris</a> in 1534.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAttwater1965141_3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAttwater1965141-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> He led an extensive mission into Asia, mainly the <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_Empire_in_the_East" class="mw-redirect" title="Portuguese Empire in the East">Portuguese Empire in the East</a>, and was influential in <a href="/wiki/Evangelisation" class="mw-redirect" title="Evangelisation">evangelisation</a> work, most notably in <a href="/wiki/Early_modern_India" class="mw-redirect" title="Early modern India">early modern India</a>. He was extensively involved in the missionary activity in <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_India" title="Portuguese India">Portuguese India</a>. In 1546, Francis Xavier proposed the establishment of the <a href="/wiki/Goan_Inquisition" class="mw-redirect" title="Goan Inquisition">Goan Inquisition</a> in a letter addressed to the Portuguese King, <a href="/wiki/John_III_of_Portugal" title="John III of Portugal">John III</a>.
<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENeill2004160_4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENeill2004160-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERao196343_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERao196343-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> While some sources claim that he actually asked for a special minister whose sole office would be to further <a href="/wiki/Christianity_in_Goa" title="Christianity in Goa">Christianity in Goa</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeridge1872268_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeridge1872268-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> others disagree with this assertion.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENeill2004160–1_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENeill2004160–1-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> He was also the first <a href="/wiki/Christian_missionary" class="mw-redirect" title="Christian missionary">Christian missionary</a> to venture into <a href="/wiki/Borneo" title="Borneo">Borneo</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Maluku_Islands" title="Maluku Islands">Maluku Islands</a>, and other areas. In those areas, struggling to learn the local languages and in the face of opposition, he had less success than what he had enjoyed in India. Xavier was about to extend his mission to <a href="/wiki/Ming_China" class="mw-redirect" title="Ming China">Ming China</a>, when he died on <a href="/wiki/Shangchuan_Island" title="Shangchuan Island">Shangchuan Island</a>.
</p><p>He was beatified by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_V" title="Pope Paul V">Pope Paul V</a> on 25 October 1619 and <a href="/wiki/Canonized" class="mw-redirect" title="Canonized">canonized</a> by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Gregory_XV" title="Pope Gregory XV">Pope Gregory XV</a> on 12 March 1622. In 1624, he was made co-patron of Navarre. Known as the "Apostle of the <a href="/wiki/Indies" class="mw-redirect" title="Indies">Indies</a>", "Apostle of <a href="/wiki/The_Far_East" class="mw-redirect" title="The Far East">the Far East</a>", "Apostle of China" and "Apostle of Japan", he is considered to be one of the greatest missionaries since <a href="/wiki/Paul_the_Apostle" title="Paul the Apostle">Paul the Apostle</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDe_Rosa200690_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDe_Rosa200690-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> In 1927, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XI" title="Pope Pius XI">Pope Pius XI</a> published the decree "Apostolicorum in Missionibus" naming Francis Xavier, along with <a href="/wiki/Th%C3%A9r%C3%A8se_of_Lisieux" title="Thérèse of Lisieux">Thérèse of Lisieux</a>, co-patron of all foreign missions.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> He is now co-patron saint of Navarre, with <a href="/wiki/Fermin" title="Fermin">Fermin</a>. The Day of Navarre in <a href="/wiki/Navarre" title="Navarre">Navarre</a>, Spain, marks the anniversary of Francis Xavier's death, on 3 December 1552.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_Xavier&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Castillo_javier.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1e/Castillo_javier.jpg/220px-Castillo_javier.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="130" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1e/Castillo_javier.jpg/330px-Castillo_javier.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1e/Castillo_javier.jpg/440px-Castillo_javier.jpg 2x" data-file-width="801" data-file-height="474" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Castillo_javier.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Castle_of_Xavier" title="Castle of Xavier">castle of the Xavier family</a> was later acquired by the Society of Jesus.</div></div></div>
<p>Francis Xavier was born in the <a href="/wiki/Castle_of_Xavier" title="Castle of Xavier">Castle of Xavier</a>, in the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Navarre" title="Kingdom of Navarre">Kingdom of Navarre</a>, on 7 April 1506 into an influential noble family. He was the youngest son of Don Juan de Jasso y Atondo, Lord of Idocín, president of the Royal Council of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Navarre" title="Kingdom of Navarre">Kingdom of Navarre</a>, and seneschal of the <a href="/wiki/Castle_of_Xavier" title="Castle of Xavier">Castle of Xavier</a> (a doctor in law by the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Bologna" title="University of Bologna">University of Bologna</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195217_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195217-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> belonging to a prosperous noble family of <a href="/wiki/Saint-Jean-Pied-de-Port" title="Saint-Jean-Pied-de-Port">Saint-Jean-Pied-de-Pont</a>, later privy counsellor and finance minister to King <a href="/wiki/John_III_of_Navarre" title="John III of Navarre">John III of Navarre</a>)<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195218_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195218-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> and Doña María de Azpilcueta y Aznárez, sole heiress to the <a href="/wiki/Castle_of_Xavier" title="Castle of Xavier">Castle of Xavier</a> (related to the theologian and philosopher <a href="/wiki/Mart%C3%ADn_de_Azpilcueta" title="Martín de Azpilcueta">Martín de Azpilcueta</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195216_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195216-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> His brother Miguel de Jasso (later known as Miguel de Javier) became Lord of Xavier and Idocín at the death of his parents (a direct ancestor of the Counts of Javier). 
</p><p>In 1512, <a href="/wiki/Ferdinand_II_of_Aragon" title="Ferdinand II of Aragon">Ferdinand</a>, King of <a href="/wiki/Crown_of_Aragon" title="Crown of Aragon">Aragon</a> and regent of <a href="/wiki/Crown_of_Castile" title="Crown of Castile">Castile</a>, invaded Navarre, initiating <a href="/wiki/Spanish_conquest_of_Iberian_Navarre" title="Spanish conquest of Iberian Navarre">a war that lasted over 18 years</a>. Three years later, Francis's father died when Francis was only nine years old. In 1516, Francis's brothers participated in a failed Navarrese-French attempt to expel the Spanish invaders from the kingdom. The Spanish Governor, Cardinal Cisneros, confiscated the family lands, demolished the outer wall, the gates, and two towers of the family castle, and filled in the moat. In addition, the height of the keep was reduced by half.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESagredo2006_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESagredo2006-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> Only the family residence inside the castle was left. In 1522, one of Francis's brothers participated with 200 Navarrese nobles in dogged but failed resistance against the Castilian Count of Miranda in Amaiur, <a href="/wiki/Baztan_(valley)" class="mw-redirect" title="Baztan (valley)">Baztan</a>, the last Navarrese territorial position south of the Pyrenees.
</p><p>In 1525, Francis went to study in Paris at the Collège Sainte-Barbe, <a href="/wiki/University_of_Paris" title="University of Paris">University of Paris</a>, where he spent the next eleven years.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195228_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195228-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> In the early days he acquired some reputation as an athlete<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195221_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195221-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> and a high-jumper.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195233_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195233-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1529, Francis shared lodgings with his friend <a href="/wiki/Pierre_Favre" class="mw-redirect" title="Pierre Favre">Pierre Favre</a>. A new student, <a href="/wiki/Ignatius_of_Loyola" title="Ignatius of Loyola">Ignatius of Loyola</a>, came to room with them.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195240_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195240-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> At 38, Ignatius was much older than Pierre and Francis, who were both 23 at the time. Ignatius convinced Pierre to become a priest, but was unable to convince Francis, who had aspirations of worldly advancement. At first, Francis regarded the new lodger as a joke and was sarcastic about his efforts to convert students.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195241_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195241-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> When Pierre left their lodgings to visit his family and Ignatius was alone with Francis, he was able to slowly break down Francis's resistance.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDe_Rosa200693_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDe_Rosa200693-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> According to most biographies Ignatius is said to have posed the question: "What will it profit a man to gain the whole world, and lose his own soul?"<sup id="cite_ref-butler_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-butler-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> However, according to <a href="/wiki/James_Patrick_Broderick" class="mw-redirect" title="James Patrick Broderick">James Broderick</a> such method is not characteristic of Ignatius and there is no evidence that he employed it at all.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195241_20-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195241-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1530, Francis received the degree of Master of Arts, and afterwards taught Aristotelian philosophy at Beauvais College, <a href="/wiki/University_of_Paris" title="University of Paris">University of Paris</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195241_20-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195241-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Missionary_work">Missionary work</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_Xavier&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Missionary work">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:StPierreParis.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/StPierreParis.jpg/220px-StPierreParis.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="293" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/StPierreParis.jpg/330px-StPierreParis.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/StPierreParis.jpg/440px-StPierreParis.jpg 2x" data-file-width="960" data-file-height="1280" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:StPierreParis.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Church of <a href="/wiki/Saint-Pierre_de_Montmartre" title="Saint-Pierre de Montmartre">Saint-Pierre de Montmartre</a>, Paris</div></div></div>
<p>On 15 August 1534, seven students met in a crypt beneath the Church of Saint Denis (now <a href="/wiki/Saint_Pierre_de_Montmartre" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Pierre de Montmartre">Saint Pierre de Montmartre</a>), on the hill of <a href="/wiki/Montmartre" title="Montmartre">Montmartre</a>, overlooking Paris. They were Francis, <a href="/wiki/Ignatius_of_Loyola" title="Ignatius of Loyola">Ignatius of Loyola</a>, <a href="/wiki/Alfonso_Salmeron" title="Alfonso Salmeron">Alfonso Salmeron</a>, <a href="/wiki/Diego_Laynez" title="Diego Laynez">Diego Laínez</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nicholas_Bobadilla" title="Nicholas Bobadilla">Nicolás Bobadilla</a> from Spain, <a href="/wiki/Peter_Faber" title="Peter Faber">Peter Faber</a> from <a href="/wiki/Savoy" title="Savoy">Savoy</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Sim%C3%A3o_Rodrigues" title="Simão Rodrigues">Simão Rodrigues</a> from <a href="/wiki/Portugal" title="Portugal">Portugal</a>. They made private vows of <a href="/wiki/Poverty,_chastity,_and_obedience" class="mw-redirect" title="Poverty, chastity, and obedience">poverty, chastity, and obedience</a> to the Pope, and also vowed to go to the Holy Land to convert infidels.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDe_Rosa200695_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDe_Rosa200695-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195247_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195247-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> Francis began his study of theology in 1534 and was ordained on 24 June 1537.
</p><p>In 1539, after long discussions, Ignatius drew up a formula for a new religious order, the <a href="/wiki/Society_of_Jesus" class="mw-redirect" title="Society of Jesus">Society of Jesus</a> (the Jesuits).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDe_Rosa200693_21-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDe_Rosa200693-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> Ignatius's plan for the order was approved by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_III" title="Pope Paul III">Pope Paul III</a> in 1540.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDe_Rosa200637_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDe_Rosa200637-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1540, <a href="/wiki/John_III_of_Portugal" title="John III of Portugal">King John of Portugal</a> had <a href="/wiki/Pedro_Mascarenhas" title="Pedro Mascarenhas">Pedro Mascarenhas</a>, Portuguese ambassador to the <a href="/wiki/Holy_See" title="Holy See">Holy See</a>, request Jesuit missionaries to spread the faith in his new possessions in India, where the king believed that Christian values were eroding among the Portuguese. After successive appeals to the Pope asking for missionaries for the <a href="/wiki/East_Indies" title="East Indies">East Indies</a> under the <a href="/wiki/Padroado" title="Padroado">Padroado</a> agreement, John III was encouraged by <a href="/wiki/Diogo_de_Gouveia" title="Diogo de Gouveia">Diogo de Gouveia</a>, rector of the <a href="/wiki/Coll%C3%A8ge_Sainte-Barbe" title="Collège Sainte-Barbe">Collège Sainte-Barbe</a>, to recruit the newly graduated students who had established the Society of Jesus.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELach199412_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELach199412-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Saint_Francis_Xavier_taking_leave_of_King_John_III_(1635)_-_Jos%C3%A9_Avelar_Rebelo.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/Saint_Francis_Xavier_taking_leave_of_King_John_III_%281635%29_-_Jos%C3%A9_Avelar_Rebelo.png/220px-Saint_Francis_Xavier_taking_leave_of_King_John_III_%281635%29_-_Jos%C3%A9_Avelar_Rebelo.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="210" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/Saint_Francis_Xavier_taking_leave_of_King_John_III_%281635%29_-_Jos%C3%A9_Avelar_Rebelo.png/330px-Saint_Francis_Xavier_taking_leave_of_King_John_III_%281635%29_-_Jos%C3%A9_Avelar_Rebelo.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/Saint_Francis_Xavier_taking_leave_of_King_John_III_%281635%29_-_Jos%C3%A9_Avelar_Rebelo.png/440px-Saint_Francis_Xavier_taking_leave_of_King_John_III_%281635%29_-_Jos%C3%A9_Avelar_Rebelo.png 2x" data-file-width="1340" data-file-height="1280" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Saint_Francis_Xavier_taking_leave_of_King_John_III_(1635)_-_Jos%C3%A9_Avelar_Rebelo.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Francisco Xavier taking leave of <a href="/wiki/John_III_of_Portugal" title="John III of Portugal">John III of Portugal</a> for an expedition</div></div></div>
<p>Ignatius promptly appointed <a href="/wiki/Nicholas_Bobadilla" title="Nicholas Bobadilla">Nicholas Bobadilla</a> and <a href="/wiki/Sim%C3%A3o_Rodrigues" title="Simão Rodrigues">Simão Rodrigues</a>. At the last moment, however, Bobadilla became seriously ill. With some hesitance and uneasiness, Ignatius asked Francis to go in Bobadilla's place. Thus, Francis Xavier began his life as the first Jesuit missionary almost accidentally.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDe_Rosa200696_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDe_Rosa200696-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195277_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195277-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-jack_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-jack-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Leaving Rome on 15 March 1540, in the Ambassador's train,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195278_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195278-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> Francis took with him a <a href="/wiki/Breviary" title="Breviary">breviary</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Catechism" title="Catechism">catechism</a>, and <span title="Latin-language text"><i lang="la">De Institutione bene vivendi</i></span> by <a href="/wiki/Croatia" title="Croatia">Croatian</a> humanist <a href="/wiki/Marko_Maruli%C4%87" title="Marko Marulić">Marko Marulić</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195296_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195296-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> a Latin book that had become popular in the <a href="/wiki/Counter-Reformation" title="Counter-Reformation">Counter-Reformation</a>. According to a 1549 letter of F. Balthasar Gago from Goa, it was the only book that Francis read or studied.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKadič196112–18_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKadič196112–18-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> Francis reached Lisbon in June 1540 and, four days after his arrival, he and Rodrigues were summoned to a private audience with the King and the Queen.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195285_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrodrick195285-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Francis Xavier devoted much of his life to missions in Asia, mainly in four centres: Malacca, Amboina and Ternate, Japan, and off-shore China. His growing information about new places indicated to him that he had to go to what he understood were centres of influence for the whole region. China loomed large from his days in India. Japan was particularly attractive because of its culture. For him, these areas were interconnected; they could not be evangelised separately.<sup id="cite_ref-japc_34-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-japc-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Goa_and_India">Goa and India</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_Xavier&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Goa and India">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:S%C3%A3o_Francisco_Xavier_pregando_em_Goa_(1610)_-_Andr%C3%A9_Reinoso_(Museu_de_S%C3%A3o_Roque).png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/S%C3%A3o_Francisco_Xavier_pregando_em_Goa_%281610%29_-_Andr%C3%A9_Reinoso_%28Museu_de_S%C3%A3o_Roque%29.png/220px-S%C3%A3o_Francisco_Xavier_pregando_em_Goa_%281610%29_-_Andr%C3%A9_Reinoso_%28Museu_de_S%C3%A3o_Roque%29.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="135" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/S%C3%A3o_Francisco_Xavier_pregando_em_Goa_%281610%29_-_Andr%C3%A9_Reinoso_%28Museu_de_S%C3%A3o_Roque%29.png/330px-S%C3%A3o_Francisco_Xavier_pregando_em_Goa_%281610%29_-_Andr%C3%A9_Reinoso_%28Museu_de_S%C3%A3o_Roque%29.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/S%C3%A3o_Francisco_Xavier_pregando_em_Goa_%281610%29_-_Andr%C3%A9_Reinoso_%28Museu_de_S%C3%A3o_Roque%29.png/440px-S%C3%A3o_Francisco_Xavier_pregando_em_Goa_%281610%29_-_Andr%C3%A9_Reinoso_%28Museu_de_S%C3%A3o_Roque%29.png 2x" data-file-width="2609" data-file-height="1602" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:S%C3%A3o_Francisco_Xavier_pregando_em_Goa_(1610)_-_Andr%C3%A9_Reinoso_(Museu_de_S%C3%A3o_Roque).png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><i>Saint Francis Xavier preaching in Goa</i> (1610), by <a href="/w/index.php?title=Andr%C3%A9_Reinoso&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="André Reinoso (page does not exist)">André Reinoso</a></div></div></div>
<p>Francis Xavier left <a href="/wiki/Lisbon" title="Lisbon">Lisbon</a> on 7 April 1541, his thirty-fifth birthday, along with two other Jesuits and the new <a href="/wiki/Viceroy" title="Viceroy">viceroy</a> <a href="/wiki/Martim_Afonso_de_Sousa" title="Martim Afonso de Sousa">Martim Afonso de Sousa</a>, on board the <i>Santiago</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrodrick1952100_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrodrick1952100-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> As he departed, Francis was given a brief from the pope appointing him <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_nuncio" class="mw-redirect" title="Apostolic nuncio">apostolic nuncio</a> to the East.<sup id="cite_ref-jack_29-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-jack-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> From August until March 1542 he remained in <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_Mozambique" title="Portuguese Mozambique">Portuguese Mozambique</a>, and arrived in <a href="/wiki/Goa" title="Goa">Goa</a>, then capital of <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_India" title="Portuguese India">Portuguese India</a>, on 6 May 1542, thirteen months after leaving Lisbon.
</p><p>The Portuguese, following quickly on the great voyages of discovery, had established themselves at Goa thirty years earlier. Francis's primary mission, as ordered by King John III, was to restore Christianity among the Portuguese settlers. According to Teotonio R. DeSouza, recent critical accounts indicate that apart from the posted civil servants, "the great majority of those who were dispatched as 'discoverers' were the riff-raff of Portuguese society, picked up from Portuguese jails."<sup id="cite_ref-grupolusofona_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-grupolusofona-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> Nor did the soldiers, sailors, or merchants come to do missionary work, and Imperial policy permitted the outflow of disaffected nobility. Many of the arrivals formed liaisons with local women and adopted Indian culture. Missionaries often wrote against the "scandalous and undisciplined" behaviour of their fellow Christians.<sup id="cite_ref-Delio_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Delio-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Christian population had churches, clergy, and a bishop, but there were few preachers and no priests beyond the walls of Goa. Xavier decided that he must begin by instructing the Portuguese themselves, and gave much of his time to the teaching of children. The first five months he spent in preaching and ministering to the sick in the hospitals.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAstrain1909_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAstrain1909-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> After that, he walked through the streets ringing a bell to summon the children and servants to catechism.<sup id="cite_ref-crawley_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-crawley-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> He was invited to head <a href="/wiki/Saint_Paul%27s_College,_Goa" title="Saint Paul&#39;s College, Goa">Saint Paul's College</a>, a pioneer <a href="/wiki/Seminary" title="Seminary">seminary</a> for the education of secular priests, which became the first Jesuit headquarters in Asia.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><b>Conversion efforts</b>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Conversion_of_Paravas_by_Francis_Xavier_in_1542.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/Conversion_of_Paravas_by_Francis_Xavier_in_1542.jpg/220px-Conversion_of_Paravas_by_Francis_Xavier_in_1542.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="285" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/f/f9/Conversion_of_Paravas_by_Francis_Xavier_in_1542.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="306" data-file-height="397" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Conversion_of_Paravas_by_Francis_Xavier_in_1542.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Conversion of the <a href="/wiki/Paravar" title="Paravar">Paravars</a> by Francis Xavier in <a href="/wiki/South_India" title="South India">South India</a>, in a 19th-century coloured lithograph</div></div></div>
<p>Xavier soon learned that along the Pearl Fishery Coast, which extends from <a href="/wiki/Cape_Comorin" class="mw-redirect" title="Cape Comorin">Cape Comorin</a> on the southern tip of India to the island of <a href="/wiki/Mannar,_Sri_Lanka" title="Mannar, Sri Lanka">Mannar</a>, off Ceylon (<a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka" title="Sri Lanka">Sri Lanka</a>), there was a <a href="/wiki/J%C4%81ti" title="Jāti">Jāti</a> of people called <a href="/wiki/Paravar" title="Paravar">Paravas</a>. Many of them had been baptised ten years before, merely to please the Portuguese who had helped them against the Moors, but remained uninstructed in the faith. Accompanied by several native clerics from the seminary at Goa, he set sail for Cape Comorin in October 1542. He taught those who had already been baptised and preached to those who weren't. His efforts with the high-caste Brahmins remained unavailing. The Brahmin and Muslim authorities in Travancore opposed Xavier with violence; time and again his hut was burned down over his head, and once he saved his life only by hiding among the branches of a large tree.<sup id="cite_ref-crawley_39-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-crawley-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He devoted almost three years to the work of preaching to the people of southern India and Ceylon, converting many. He built nearly 40 churches along the coast, including <a href="/wiki/St._Stephen%27s_Church,_Kombuthurai" title="St. Stephen&#39;s Church, Kombuthurai">St. Stephen's Church, Kombuthurai</a>, mentioned in his letters dated 1544.
</p><p>During this time, he was able to visit the tomb of <a href="/wiki/Thomas_the_Apostle" title="Thomas the Apostle">Thomas the Apostle</a> in <a href="/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Tom%C3%A9_de_Meliapore" title="São Tomé de Meliapore">Mylapore</a> (now part of Madras/<a href="/wiki/Chennai" title="Chennai">Chennai</a> then in Portuguese India).<sup id="cite_ref-jack_29-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-jack-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> He set his sights eastward in 1545 and planned a missionary journey to <a href="/wiki/Makassar" title="Makassar">Makassar</a> on the island of <a href="/wiki/Sulawesi" title="Sulawesi">Celebes</a> (today's <a href="/wiki/Indonesia" title="Indonesia">Indonesia</a>).
</p><p>As the first Jesuit in India, Francis had difficulty achieving much success in his missionary trips. His successors, such as de Nobili, <a href="/wiki/Matteo_Ricci" title="Matteo Ricci">Matteo Ricci</a>, and Beschi, attempted to convert the noblemen first as a means to influence more people, while Francis had initially interacted most with the lower classes; (later though, in Japan, Francis changed tack by paying tribute to the Emperor and seeking an audience with him).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDuignan1958725–732_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDuignan1958725–732-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:302px;"><a href="/wiki/File:St._Francis_Xavier_-_Asia_Voyages.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e0/St._Francis_Xavier_-_Asia_Voyages.svg/300px-St._Francis_Xavier_-_Asia_Voyages.svg.png" decoding="async" width="300" height="193" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e0/St._Francis_Xavier_-_Asia_Voyages.svg/450px-St._Francis_Xavier_-_Asia_Voyages.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e0/St._Francis_Xavier_-_Asia_Voyages.svg/600px-St._Francis_Xavier_-_Asia_Voyages.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1400" data-file-height="900" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:St._Francis_Xavier_-_Asia_Voyages.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Voyages of Saint Francis Xavier</div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Southeast_Asia">Southeast Asia</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_Xavier&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Southeast Asia">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In the spring of 1545 Xavier started for <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_Malacca" title="Portuguese Malacca">Portuguese Malacca</a>. He laboured there for the last months of that year. About January 1546, Xavier left Malacca for the <a href="/wiki/Maluku_Islands" title="Maluku Islands">Maluku Islands</a>, where the Portuguese had some settlements. For a year and a half, he preached the Gospel there. He went first to <a href="/wiki/Ambon_Island" title="Ambon Island">Ambon Island</a>, where he stayed until mid-June. He then visited the other Maluku Islands, including <a href="/wiki/Ternate" title="Ternate">Ternate</a>, Baranura, and <a href="/wiki/Morotai" title="Morotai">Morotai</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAstrain1909_38-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAstrain1909-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> Shortly after Easter 1547, he returned to Ambon Island; a few months later he returned to Malacca.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Japan">Japan</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_Xavier&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Japan">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/History_of_the_Catholic_Church_in_Japan" title="History of the Catholic Church in Japan">History of the Catholic Church in Japan</a></div>
<p>In Malacca in December 1547, Francis Xavier met a Japanese man named <a href="/wiki/Anjir%C5%8D" title="Anjirō">Anjirō</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAstrain1909_38-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAstrain1909-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> Anjirō had heard of Francis in 1545 and had travelled from <a href="/wiki/Kagoshima" title="Kagoshima">Kagoshima</a> to Malacca to meet him. Having been charged with murder, Anjirō had fled Japan. He told Francis extensively about his former life, and the customs and culture of his homeland. Anjirō became the first Japanese Christian and adopted the name 'Paulo de Santa Fé'. He later helped Xavier as a mediator and interpreter for the mission to Japan that now seemed much more possible.
</p><p>In January 1548 Francis returned to Goa to attend to his responsibilities as superior of the mission there.<sup id="cite_ref-wintz_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-wintz-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> The next 15 months were occupied with various journeys and administrative measures. He left Goa on 15 April 1549, stopped at Malacca, and visited <a href="/wiki/Guangzhou" title="Guangzhou">Canton</a>. He was accompanied by Anjiro, two other Japanese men, Father <a href="/wiki/Cosme_de_Torr%C3%A8s" class="mw-redirect" title="Cosme de Torrès">Cosme de Torrès</a> and Brother <a href="/wiki/Juan_Fern%C3%A1ndez_(missionary)" title="Juan Fernández (missionary)">Juan Fernández</a>. He had taken with him presents for the "<a href="/wiki/Emperor_of_Japan" title="Emperor of Japan">King of Japan</a>" since he was intending to introduce himself as the <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Nuncio" class="mw-redirect" title="Apostolic Nuncio">Apostolic Nuncio</a>.
</p><p>Europeans had already come to Japan: the Portuguese had landed in 1543 on the island of <a href="/wiki/Tanegashima" title="Tanegashima">Tanegashima</a>, where they introduced matchlock firearms to Japan.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPacheco1974477–480_43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPacheco1974477–480-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>From Amboina, he wrote to his companions in Europe: "I asked a Portuguese merchant, ... who had been for many days in Anjirō's country of Japan, to give me ... some information on that land and its people from what he had seen and heard. ...All the Portuguese merchants coming from Japan tell me that if I go there I shall do great service for God our Lord, more than with the pagans of India, for they are a very reasonable people." (To His Companions Residing in Rome, From Cochin, 20 January 1548, no. 18, p.&#160;178).<sup id="cite_ref-japc_34-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-japc-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Francis Xavier reached Japan on 27 July 1549, with Anjiro and three other Jesuits, but he was not permitted to enter any port his ship arrived at until 15 August,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPacheco1974477–480_43-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPacheco1974477–480-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> when he went ashore at <a href="/wiki/Kagoshima" title="Kagoshima">Kagoshima</a>, the principal port of <a href="/wiki/Satsuma_Province" title="Satsuma Province">Satsuma Province</a> on the island of <a href="/wiki/Ky%C5%ABsh%C5%AB" class="mw-redirect" title="Kyūshū">Kyūshū</a>. As a representative of the Portuguese king, he was received in a friendly manner. <a href="/wiki/Shimazu_Takahisa" title="Shimazu Takahisa">Shimazu Takahisa</a> (1514–1571), <i><a href="/wiki/Daimy%C5%8D" class="mw-redirect" title="Daimyō">daimyō</a></i> of Satsuma, gave a friendly reception to Francis on 29 September 1549, but in the following year he forbade the conversion of his subjects to Christianity under penalty of death; Christians in Kagoshima could not be given any catechism in the following years. The Portuguese missionary Pedro de Alcáçova would later write in 1554:
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>In Cangoxima, the first place Father Master Francisco stopped at, there were a good number of Christians, although there was no one there to teach them; the shortage of labourers prevented the whole kingdom from becoming Christian.</p><div class="templatequotecite">—&#8201;<cite><a href="#CITEREFPacheco1974">Pacheco 1974</a>, pp.&#160;477–480</cite></div></blockquote>
<p>Francis was the first Jesuit to go to <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.abwe.org/serve/countries/japan">Japan as a missionary</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> He brought with him paintings of the <a href="/wiki/Madonna_and_Child_(Duccio,_Metropolitan)" title="Madonna and Child (Duccio, Metropolitan)">Madonna and the Madonna and Child</a>. These paintings were used to help teach the Japanese about Christianity. There was a huge language barrier as <a href="/wiki/Japanese_language" title="Japanese language">Japanese</a> was unlike other languages the missionaries had previously encountered. For a long time, Francis struggled to learn the language.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (March 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> He was hosted by Anjirō's family until October 1550.<sup id="cite_ref-butler_22-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-butler-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> From October to December 1550, he resided in <a href="/wiki/Yamaguchi,_Yamaguchi" class="mw-redirect" title="Yamaguchi, Yamaguchi">Yamaguchi</a>. Shortly before Christmas, he left for <a href="/wiki/Kyoto" title="Kyoto">Kyoto</a> but failed to meet with the Emperor. He returned to Yamaguchi in March 1551, where the daimyo of the province gave him permission to preach.
</p><p>Having learned that evangelical poverty did not have the appeal in Japan that it had in Europe and in India, he decided to change his approach. Hearing after a time that a Portuguese ship had arrived at a port in the province of Bungo in Kyushu and that the prince there would like to see him, Xavier now set out southward. The Jesuit, in a fine cassock, surplice, and stole, was attended by thirty gentlemen and as many servants, all in their best clothes. Five of them bore on cushions valuable articles, including a portrait of Our Lady and a pair of velvet slippers, these not gifts for the prince, but solemn offerings to Xavier, to impress the onlookers with his eminence. Handsomely dressed, with his companions acting as attendants, he presented himself before Oshindono, the ruler of Nagate, and as a representative of the great kingdom of Portugal, offered him letters and presents: a musical instrument, a watch, and other attractive objects which had been given him by the authorities in India for the emperor.<sup id="cite_ref-crawley_39-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-crawley-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>For forty-five years the Jesuits were the only missionaries in Asia, but the Franciscans also began proselytising in Asia as well. Christian missionaries were later forced into exile, along with their assistants. However, some were able to stay behind. Christianity was then kept underground so as to not be persecuted.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Japanese people were not easily converted; many of the people were already <a href="/wiki/Buddhism" title="Buddhism">Buddhist</a> or <a href="/wiki/Shinto" title="Shinto">Shinto</a>. Francis tried to combat the disposition of some of the Japanese that a God who had created everything, including evil, could not be good. The concept of Hell was also a struggle; the Japanese were bothered by the idea of their ancestors living in Hell. Despite Francis's different religion, he felt that they were good people, much like Europeans, and could be converted.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-fordham_48-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-fordham-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Xavier was welcomed by the <a href="/wiki/Shingon" class="mw-redirect" title="Shingon">Shingon</a> monks since he used the word <i><a href="/wiki/Vairocana" title="Vairocana">Dainichi</a></i> for the Christian God; attempting to adapt the concept to local traditions. As Xavier learned more about the religious nuances of the word, he changed to <i>Deusu</i><sup id="cite_ref-butler_22-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-butler-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> from the Latin and Portuguese <i>Deus</i>. The monks later realised that Xavier was preaching a rival religion and grew more aggressive towards his attempts at conversion.
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Nasugbu_31_(New_Church_Altar).JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/3/34/Nasugbu_31_%28New_Church_Altar%29.JPG/220px-Nasugbu_31_%28New_Church_Altar%29.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/3/34/Nasugbu_31_%28New_Church_Altar%29.JPG/330px-Nasugbu_31_%28New_Church_Altar%29.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/3/34/Nasugbu_31_%28New_Church_Altar%29.JPG/440px-Nasugbu_31_%28New_Church_Altar%29.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2032" data-file-height="1524" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Nasugbu_31_(New_Church_Altar).JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The Altar of St. Francis Xavier Parish in <a href="/wiki/Nasugbu,_Batangas" class="mw-redirect" title="Nasugbu, Batangas">Nasugbu, Batangas</a>, Philippines. Saint Francis is the principal patron of the town, together with <a href="/wiki/Mary_(mother_of_Jesus)" class="mw-redirect" title="Mary (mother of Jesus)">Our Lady of Escalera</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>With the passage of time, his sojourn in Japan could be considered somewhat fruitful as attested by congregations established in <a href="/wiki/Hirado" class="mw-redirect" title="Hirado">Hirado</a>, Yamaguchi, and <a href="/wiki/Bungo_Province" title="Bungo Province">Bungo</a>. Xavier worked for more than two years in Japan and saw his successor-Jesuits established. He then decided to return to India. Historians debate the exact path by which he returned, but from evidence attributed to the captain of his ship, he may have travelled through Tanegeshima and Minato, and avoided Kagoshima because of the hostility of the daimyo.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPacheco1974477–480_43-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPacheco1974477–480-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="China">China</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_Xavier&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: China">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>During his trip from Japan back to India, a tempest forced him to stop on an island near <a href="/wiki/Guangzhou" title="Guangzhou">Guangzhou</a>, <a href="/wiki/Guangdong" title="Guangdong">Guangdong</a>, China, where he met Diogo Pereira, a rich merchant and an old friend from <a href="/wiki/Kochi" title="Kochi">Cochin</a>. Pereira showed him a letter from Portuguese prisoners in Guangzhou, asking for a Portuguese ambassador to speak to the Chinese Emperor on their behalf. Later during the voyage, he stopped at Malacca on 27 December 1551 and was back in Goa by January 1552.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (March 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>On 17 April he set sail with Diogo Pereira on the <i>Santa Cruz</i> for China. He planned to introduce himself as Apostolic Nuncio and Pereira as the ambassador of the King of Portugal. But then he realized that he had forgotten his testimonial letters as an Apostolic Nuncio. Back in Malacca, he was confronted by the captain Álvaro de Ataíde da Gama who now had total control over the harbour. The captain refused to recognize his title of Nuncio, asked Pereira to resign from his title of ambassador, named a new crew for the ship, and demanded the gifts for the Chinese Emperor be left in Malacca.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (March 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In late August 1552, the <i>Santa Cruz</i> reached the Chinese island of <a href="/wiki/Shangchuan" class="mw-redirect" title="Shangchuan">Shangchuan</a>, 14&#160;km away from the southern coast of mainland China, near <a href="/wiki/Taishan,_Guangdong" title="Taishan, Guangdong">Taishan</a>, Guangdong, 200&#160;km south-west of what later became <a href="/wiki/Hong_Kong" title="Hong Kong">Hong Kong</a>. At this time, he was accompanied only by a Jesuit student, Álvaro Ferreira, a Chinese man called António, and a <a href="/wiki/Malabars" title="Malabars">Malabar</a> servant called Christopher. Around mid-November, he sent a letter saying that a man had agreed to take him to the mainland in exchange for a large sum of money. Having sent back Álvaro Ferreira, he remained alone with António. He died from a fever at Shangchuan, Taishan, China, on 3 December 1552, while he was waiting for a boat that would take him to mainland China.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Burials_and_relics">Burials and relics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_Xavier&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Burials and relics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Casket_of_Saint_Francis_Xavier.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Casket_of_Saint_Francis_Xavier.jpg/170px-Casket_of_Saint_Francis_Xavier.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="254" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Casket_of_Saint_Francis_Xavier.jpg/255px-Casket_of_Saint_Francis_Xavier.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Casket_of_Saint_Francis_Xavier.jpg/340px-Casket_of_Saint_Francis_Xavier.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2374" data-file-height="3552" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Casket_of_Saint_Francis_Xavier.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Casket of Saint Francis Xavier in the <a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_Bom_Jesus" title="Basilica of Bom Jesus">Basilica of Bom Jesus</a> in <a href="/wiki/Goa" title="Goa">Goa</a>, <a href="/wiki/India" title="India">India</a></div></div></div>
<p>Xavier was first buried on a beach at <a href="/wiki/Shangchuan_Island" title="Shangchuan Island">Shangchuan Island</a>, <a href="/wiki/Taishan,_Guangdong" title="Taishan, Guangdong">Taishan</a>, Guangdong. His body was taken from the island in February 1553 and temporarily buried in <a href="/wiki/St._Paul%27s_Church,_Melaka" class="mw-redirect" title="St. Paul&#39;s Church, Melaka">St. Paul's Church</a> in <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_Malacca" title="Portuguese Malacca">Portuguese Malacca</a> on 22 March 1553. An open grave in the church now marks the place of Xavier's burial. Pereira came back from Goa, removed the corpse shortly after 15 April 1553, and moved it to his house. On 11 December 1553, Xavier's body was shipped to Goa.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The body is now in the <a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_Bom_Jesus" title="Basilica of Bom Jesus">Basilica of Bom Jesus</a> in Goa, where it was placed in a glass container encased in a silver casket on 2 December 1637.<sup id="cite_ref-Gesù_51-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gesù-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> This casket, constructed by Goan silversmiths between 1636 and 1637, was an exemplary blend of Italian and Indian aesthetic sensibilities. There are 32 silver plates on all four sides of the casket, depicting different episodes from the life of Xavier:
</p>
<ul><li>Francis lies on the ground with his arms and legs tied, but the cords break miraculously.</li>
<li>Francis kisses the ulcer of a patient in a Venetian hospital.</li>
<li>He is visited by Jerom as he lies ailing in the hospital of Vicenza.</li>
<li>A vision about his future apostolate.</li>
<li>A vision about his sister's prophecy about his fate.</li>
<li>He saves the secretary of the Portuguese Ambassador while crossing the Alps.</li>
<li>He lifts a sick man who dies after receiving communion but is freed from fever.</li>
<li>He baptises in Travancore.</li>
<li>He resuscitates a boy who died in a well at Cape Comorin.</li>
<li>He cures miraculously a man full of sores.</li>
<li>He drives away the Badagas in Travancore.</li>
<li>He resuscitates three persons: a man who was buried at Coulao; a boy about to be buried at Multao; and a child.</li>
<li>He takes money from his empty pockets and gives it to a Portuguese at Malyapore.</li>
<li>A miraculous cure.</li>
<li>A crab restores his crucifix which had fallen into the sea.</li>
<li>He preaches in the island of Moro.</li>
<li>He preaches in the sea of Malacca and announces the victory against the enemies.</li>
<li>He converts a Portuguese soldier.</li>
<li>He helps the dying Vicar of Malacca.</li>
<li>Francis kneels down and on his shoulders there rests a child whom he restores to health.</li>
<li>He goes from Amanguchi to Macao walking.</li>
<li>He cures a mute or unable to speak and paralytic man in Amanguchi.</li>
<li>He cures a deaf Japanese person.</li>
<li>He prays in the ship during a storm.</li>
<li>He baptises three kings in Cochin.</li>
<li>He cures a religious in the college of St. Paul.</li>
<li>Due to the lack of water, he sweetens the seawater during a voyage.</li>
<li>The agony of Francis at Sancian.</li>
<li>After his death, he is seen by a lady according to his promise.</li>
<li>The body dressed in sacerdotal vestments is exposed for public veneration.</li>
<li>Francis levitates as he distributes communion in the College of St. Paul.</li>
<li>The body is placed in a niche at Chaul with lighted candles. On the top of this casket, there is a cross with two angels. One is holding a burning heart and the other a legend which says, "Satis est Domine, satis est." (<i>It's enough Lord, it's enough</i>)</li></ul>
<p>The right <a href="/wiki/Forearm" title="Forearm">forearm</a>, which Xavier used to bless and baptise his converts, was detached by <a href="/wiki/Superior_General_of_the_Society_of_Jesus" title="Superior General of the Society of Jesus">Superior General</a> <a href="/wiki/Claudio_Acquaviva" title="Claudio Acquaviva">Claudio Acquaviva</a> in 1614. It has been displayed since in a silver reliquary at the main Jesuit church in Rome, <a href="/wiki/Il_Ges%C3%B9" class="mw-redirect" title="Il Gesù">Il Gesù</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti">.mw-parser-output .tmulti .multiimageinner{display:flex;flex-direction:column}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .trow{display:flex;flex-direction:row;clear:left;flex-wrap:wrap;width:100%;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .tsingle{margin:1px;float:left}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .theader{clear:both;font-weight:bold;text-align:center;align-self:center;background-color:transparent;width:100%}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .thumbcaption{background-color:transparent}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .text-align-left{text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .text-align-right{text-align:right}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .text-align-center{text-align:center}@media all and (max-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .tmulti .thumbinner{width:100%!important;box-sizing:border-box;max-width:none!important;align-items:center}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .trow{justify-content:center}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .tsingle{float:none!important;max-width:100%!important;box-sizing:border-box;text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .tsingle .thumbcaption{text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .trow>.thumbcaption{text-align:center}}</style><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:430px;max-width:430px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:154px;max-width:154px"><div class="thumbimage"><a
