height="203" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d5/Reliquary_of_St._Francis_Xavier%27s_humerus.jpg/228px-Reliquary_of_St._Francis_Xavier%27s_humerus.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d5/Reliquary_of_St._Francis_Xavier%27s_humerus.jpg/304px-Reliquary_of_St._Francis_Xavier%27s_humerus.jpg 2x" data-file-width="768" data-file-height="1024" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption">Saint Francis Xavier's <a href="/wiki/Humerus" title="Humerus">humerus</a> at <a href="/wiki/St._Joseph%27s_Seminary_and_Church" title="St. Joseph&#39;s Seminary and Church">St. Joseph's Church</a>, <a href="/wiki/Macao" class="mw-redirect" title="Macao">Macao</a> (2008)</div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:272px;max-width:272px"><div class="thumbimage"><a href="/wiki/File:Copy_of_Humerus_of_St._Francis_Xavier_sign.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Copy_of_Humerus_of_St._Francis_Xavier_sign.JPG/270px-Copy_of_Humerus_of_St._Francis_Xavier_sign.JPG" decoding="async" width="270" height="203" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Copy_of_Humerus_of_St._Francis_Xavier_sign.JPG/405px-Copy_of_Humerus_of_St._Francis_Xavier_sign.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Copy_of_Humerus_of_St._Francis_Xavier_sign.JPG/540px-Copy_of_Humerus_of_St._Francis_Xavier_sign.JPG 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="768" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption">Sign accompanying Saint Francis Xavier's humerus</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>Another of Xavier's arm bones was brought to <a href="/wiki/Colonial_Macau" class="mw-redirect" title="Colonial Macau">Macau</a> where it was kept in a silver <a href="/wiki/Reliquary" title="Reliquary">reliquary</a>. The relic was destined for Japan but religious persecution there persuaded the church to keep it in Macau's <a href="/wiki/Ruins_of_St._Paul" class="mw-redirect" title="Ruins of St. Paul">Cathedral of St. Paul</a>. It was subsequently moved to <a href="/wiki/St._Joseph%27s_Seminary_and_Church" title="St. Joseph&#39;s Seminary and Church">St. Joseph's</a> and in 1978 to the <a href="/wiki/Chapel_of_St._Francis_Xavier" title="Chapel of St. Francis Xavier">Chapel of St. Francis Xavier</a> on <a href="/wiki/Coloane" title="Coloane">Coloane Island</a>. More recently the relic was moved to St. Joseph's Church.<sup id="cite_ref-Macau_53-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Macau-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2006, on the 500th anniversary of his birth, the Xavier Tomb Monument and Chapel on Shangchuan Island, in ruins after years of neglect under communist rule in China, was restored with support from the alumni of <a href="/wiki/Wah_Yan_College,_Hong_Kong" title="Wah Yan College, Hong Kong">Wah Yan College</a>, a Jesuit high school in Hong Kong.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>From December 2017 to February 2018, <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Christian_Outreach" title="Catholic Christian Outreach">Catholic Christian Outreach</a> (CCO) in cooperation with the Jesuits, and the Archdiocese of Ottawa (Canada) brought Xavier's right forearm to tour throughout Canada. The faithful, especially university students participating with CCO at <a href="/wiki/Rise_Up_(conference)" title="Rise Up (conference)">Rise Up 2017</a> in Ottawa, venerated the relics. The tour continued to every city where CCO and/or the Jesuits are present in Canada: Quebec City, St. John's, Halifax, St. Francis Xavier University in <a href="/wiki/Antigonish,_Nova_Scotia" title="Antigonish, Nova Scotia">Antigonish</a> (neither CCO nor the Jesuits are present here), Kingston, Toronto, Winnipeg, Saskatoon, Regina, Calgary, Vancouver, Victoria, and Montreal before returning to Ottawa.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> The relic was then returned to Rome with a Mass of Thanksgiving celebrated by <a href="/wiki/Terrence_Prendergast" title="Terrence Prendergast">Archbishop Terrence Prendergast</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Church_of_the_Ges%C3%B9" title="Church of the Gesù">Church of the Gesu</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Veneration">Veneration</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_Xavier&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Veneration">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Beatification_and_canonization">Beatification and canonization</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_Xavier&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Beatification and canonization">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Francis Xavier was beatified by <a href="/wiki/Paul_V" class="mw-redirect" title="Paul V">Paul V</a> on 25 October 1619, and was <a href="/wiki/Canonized" class="mw-redirect" title="Canonized">canonized</a> by <a href="/wiki/Gregory_XV" class="mw-redirect" title="Gregory XV">Gregory XV</a> on 12 March<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> 1622, at the same time as <a href="/wiki/Ignatius_Loyola" class="mw-redirect" title="Ignatius Loyola">Ignatius Loyola</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> Pius XI proclaimed him the "Patron of Catholic Missions".<sup id="cite_ref-benedict_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-benedict-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> His <a href="/wiki/Feast_day" class="mw-redirect" title="Feast day">feast day</a> is 3 December.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAttwater1965141–142_58-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAttwater1965141–142-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Pilgrimage_centres">Pilgrimage centres</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_Xavier&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Pilgrimage centres">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Stain_glass_window_of_St_Francis_of_Xavier_in_B%C3%A9thanie.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/Stain_glass_window_of_St_Francis_of_Xavier_in_B%C3%A9thanie.JPG/170px-Stain_glass_window_of_St_Francis_of_Xavier_in_B%C3%A9thanie.JPG" decoding="async" width="170" height="293" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/Stain_glass_window_of_St_Francis_of_Xavier_in_B%C3%A9thanie.JPG/255px-Stain_glass_window_of_St_Francis_of_Xavier_in_B%C3%A9thanie.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/Stain_glass_window_of_St_Francis_of_Xavier_in_B%C3%A9thanie.JPG/340px-Stain_glass_window_of_St_Francis_of_Xavier_in_B%C3%A9thanie.JPG 2x" data-file-width="516" data-file-height="889" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Stain_glass_window_of_St_Francis_of_Xavier_in_B%C3%A9thanie.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Stained_glass" title="Stained glass">Stained glass</a> church window in <a href="/wiki/B%C3%A9thanie_(Hong_Kong)" title="Béthanie (Hong Kong)">Béthanie, Hong Kong</a>, of St Francis Xavier baptizing a Chinese man</div></div></div>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Goa">Goa</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_Xavier&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Goa">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Saint Francis Xavier's relics are kept in a silver casket, elevated inside the <a href="/wiki/Bom_Jesus_Basilica" class="mw-redirect" title="Bom Jesus Basilica">Bom Jesus Basilica</a> and are exposed (being brought to ground level) generally every ten years, but this is discretionary. The sacred relics went on display starting on 22 November 2014 at the XVII Solemn Exposition. The display closed on 4 January 2015.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> The previous exposition, the sixteenth, was held from 21 November 2004 to 2 January 2005.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Relics of Saint Francis Xavier are also found in the Espirito Santo (Holy Spirit) Church, <a href="/wiki/Marg%C3%A3o" class="mw-redirect" title="Margão">Margão</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> in Sanv Fransiku Xavierachi Igorz (Church of St. Francis Xavier), <a href="/w/index.php?title=Batpal&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Batpal (page does not exist)">Batpal</a>, <a href="/wiki/Canacona" title="Canacona">Canacona</a>, Goa,<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> and at St. Francis Xavier Chapel, Portais, Panjim.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Other_places">Other places</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_Xavier&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Other places">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Other pilgrimage centres include <a href="/wiki/Castle_of_Xavier" title="Castle of Xavier">Xavier's birthplace</a> in Navarra;<sup id="cite_ref-CTV_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CTV-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> the <a href="/wiki/Church_of_the_Ges%C3%B9" title="Church of the Gesù">Church of the Gesù</a>, Rome;<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> Malacca (where he was buried for two years, before being brought to Goa);<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> and Sancian (place of death).<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Xavier is a major venerated saint in both Sonora and the neighbouring U.S. state of <a href="/wiki/Arizona" title="Arizona">Arizona</a>. In <a href="/wiki/Magdalena_de_Kino" title="Magdalena de Kino">Magdalena de Kino</a> in <a href="/wiki/Sonora" title="Sonora">Sonora</a>, Mexico, in the Church of Santa María Magdalena, there is a reclining statue of San Francisco Xavier brought by pioneer Jesuit missionary Padre <a href="/wiki/Eusebio_Kino" title="Eusebio Kino">Eusebio Kino</a> in the early 18th century. The statue is said to be miraculous and is the object of pilgrimage for many in the region.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> Also the <a href="/wiki/Mission_San_Xavier_del_Bac" title="Mission San Xavier del Bac">Mission San Xavier del Bac</a> is a pilgrimage site.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> The mission is an active parish church ministering to the people of the San Xavier District, Tohono O'odham Nation, and nearby Tucson, Arizona.
</p><p>Francis Xavier is honored in the <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_(Church_of_England)" title="Calendar of saints (Church of England)">Church of England</a> and in the <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_(Episcopal_Church)" title="Calendar of saints (Episcopal Church)">Episcopal Church</a> on 3 December.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Novena_of_grace">Novena of grace</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_Xavier&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Novena of grace">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Novena_of_Grace" title="Novena of Grace">Novena of Grace</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Unzenhell3.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Unzenhell3.jpg/170px-Unzenhell3.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="118" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Unzenhell3.jpg/255px-Unzenhell3.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Unzenhell3.jpg/340px-Unzenhell3.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1960" data-file-height="1360" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Unzenhell3.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Fumaroles at Mt. Unzen, Japan</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Novena_of_Grace" title="Novena of Grace">Novena of Grace</a> is a popular devotion to Francis Xavier, typically prayed either on the nine days before 3 December, or on 4 March through 12 March (the anniversary of Pope Gregory XV's canonisation of Xavier in 1622). It began with the Italian Jesuit missionary <a href="/wiki/Marcello_Mastrilli" title="Marcello Mastrilli">Marcello Mastrilli</a>. Before he could travel to the Far East, Mastrilli was gravely injured in a freak accident after a festive celebration dedicated to the <a href="/wiki/Immaculate_Conception" title="Immaculate Conception">Immaculate Conception</a> in Naples. Delirious and on the verge of death, Mastrilli saw Xavier, who he later said asked him to choose between travelling or death by holding the respective symbols, to which Mastrilli answered, "I choose that which God wills."<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> Upon regaining his health, Mastrilli made his way via Goa and the Philippines to Satsuma, Japan. The <a href="/wiki/Tokugawa_shogunate" title="Tokugawa shogunate">Tokugawa shogunate</a> beheaded the missionary in October 1637, after undergoing three days of tortures involving the volcanic sulphurous fumes from <a href="/wiki/Mount_Unzen" title="Mount Unzen">Mt. Unzen</a>, known as the <i>Hell mouth</i> or "pit" that had supposedly caused an earlier missionary to renounce his faith.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Legacy">Legacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_Xavier&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Legacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:VisionStFrancisBoccacio.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/VisionStFrancisBoccacio.jpg/170px-VisionStFrancisBoccacio.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="234" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/VisionStFrancisBoccacio.jpg/255px-VisionStFrancisBoccacio.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/VisionStFrancisBoccacio.jpg/340px-VisionStFrancisBoccacio.jpg 2x" data-file-width="870" data-file-height="1200" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:VisionStFrancisBoccacio.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><i>The Vision of St. Francis Xavier</i>, by <a href="/wiki/Giovanni_Battista_Gaulli" title="Giovanni Battista Gaulli">Giovanni Battista Gaulli</a></div></div></div>
<p>Francis Xavier became widely noteworthy for his <a href="/wiki/Missionary_work" class="mw-redirect" title="Missionary work">missionary work</a>, both as an organiser and as a pioneer; he reputedly converted more people than anyone else had done since <a href="/wiki/Paul_the_Apostle" title="Paul the Apostle">Paul the Apostle</a>. In 2006 <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Pope Benedict XVI">Pope Benedict XVI</a> said of both <a href="/wiki/Ignatius_of_Loyola" title="Ignatius of Loyola">Ignatius of Loyola</a> and Francis Xavier: "not only their history which was interwoven for many years from <a href="/wiki/Paris" title="Paris">Paris</a> and Rome, but a unique desire – a unique passion, it could be said – moved and sustained them through different human events: the passion to give to God-Trinity a glory always greater and to work for the proclamation of the Gospel of Christ to the peoples who had been ignored."<sup id="cite_ref-benedict_57-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-benedict-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> By consulting with the earlier ancient <a href="/wiki/Christians_of_St._Thomas" class="mw-redirect" title="Christians of St. Thomas">Christians of St. Thomas</a> in India, Xavier developed Jesuit missionary methods. His success also spurred many Europeans to join the Jesuit order, as well as to become missionaries throughout the world.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (March 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> His personal efforts most affected religious practice in India and in the <a href="/wiki/East_Indies" title="East Indies">East Indies</a> (<a href="/wiki/Indonesia" title="Indonesia">Indonesia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Malaysia" title="Malaysia">Malaysia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Timor" title="Timor">Timor</a>). As of 2021<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Francis_Xavier&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup> India still has numerous Jesuit missions and many more schools. Xavier also worked to propagate Christianity in <a href="/wiki/China" title="China">China</a> and <a href="/wiki/Japan" title="Japan">Japan</a>. However, following the persecutions (1587 onwards) instituted by <a href="/wiki/Toyotomi_Hideyoshi" title="Toyotomi Hideyoshi">Toyotomi Hideyoshi</a> and the subsequent closing of Japan to foreigners (1633 onwards), the <a href="/wiki/Kakure_Kirishitan" title="Kakure Kirishitan">Christians of Japan had to go underground</a> to preserve an independent Christian culture.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> Likewise, while Xavier inspired many missionaries to China, Chinese Christians also were forced underground there and developed their own Christian culture.
</p><p>A small chapel designed by <a href="/wiki/Achille-Antoine_Hermitte" title="Achille-Antoine Hermitte">Achille-Antoine Hermitte</a> was completed in 1869 over Xavier's death-place on Shangchuan Island, Canton.
It was damaged and restored several times; the most recent restoration in 2006 marked the 500th anniversary of the saint's birth.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDavies201692–110_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavies201692–110-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Francis Xavier is the patron saint of his native <a href="/wiki/Navarre" title="Navarre">Navarre</a>, which celebrates his feast day on 3 December as a government holiday.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> In addition to Roman Catholic Masses remembering Xavier on that day (now known as the Day of Navarra), celebrations in the surrounding weeks honour the region's cultural heritage. Furthermore, in the 1940s, devoted Catholics instituted the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Javierada&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Javierada (page does not exist)">Javierada</a>, an annual day-long pilgrimage (often on foot) from the capital at <a href="/wiki/Pamplona" title="Pamplona">Pamplona</a> to Xavier, where the Jesuits built a basilica and museum and restored Francis Xavier's family's castle.<sup id="cite_ref-CTV_64-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CTV-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Personal_names">Personal names</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_Xavier&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Personal names">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Saint_Francis_Xavier_statue,_Jakarta_Cathedral,_Indonesia.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/Saint_Francis_Xavier_statue%2C_Jakarta_Cathedral%2C_Indonesia.jpg/170px-Saint_Francis_Xavier_statue%2C_Jakarta_Cathedral%2C_Indonesia.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="302" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/Saint_Francis_Xavier_statue%2C_Jakarta_Cathedral%2C_Indonesia.jpg/255px-Saint_Francis_Xavier_statue%2C_Jakarta_Cathedral%2C_Indonesia.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/Saint_Francis_Xavier_statue%2C_Jakarta_Cathedral%2C_Indonesia.jpg/340px-Saint_Francis_Xavier_statue%2C_Jakarta_Cathedral%2C_Indonesia.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2340" data-file-height="4160" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Saint_Francis_Xavier_statue,_Jakarta_Cathedral,_Indonesia.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Statue of <i>Santo Fransiskus Xaverius</i>, at Jesuit <i><a href="/wiki/Jakarta_Cathedral" title="Jakarta Cathedral">Gereja Katedral Santa Perawan Maria Diangkat Ke Surga</a></i>, in <a href="/wiki/Jakarta" title="Jakarta">Jakarta</a>, <a href="/wiki/Indonesia" title="Indonesia">Indonesia</a></div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Statue_of_Staint_Francis_Xavier.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/80/Statue_of_Staint_Francis_Xavier.JPG/170px-Statue_of_Staint_Francis_Xavier.JPG" decoding="async" width="170" height="227" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/80/Statue_of_Staint_Francis_Xavier.JPG/255px-Statue_of_Staint_Francis_Xavier.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/80/Statue_of_Staint_Francis_Xavier.JPG/340px-Statue_of_Staint_Francis_Xavier.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2448" data-file-height="3264" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Statue_of_Staint_Francis_Xavier.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Statue of Saint Francis Xavier, at St. Francis Xavier Catholic Church, in <a href="/wiki/Superior,_Wisconsin" title="Superior, Wisconsin">Superior, Wisconsin</a>, <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a></div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:S%C3%A3o_Francisco_Xavier_-_Padr%C3%A3o_dos_Descobrimentos.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a5/S%C3%A3o_Francisco_Xavier_-_Padr%C3%A3o_dos_Descobrimentos.png/170px-S%C3%A3o_Francisco_Xavier_-_Padr%C3%A3o_dos_Descobrimentos.png" decoding="async" width="170" height="243" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a5/S%C3%A3o_Francisco_Xavier_-_Padr%C3%A3o_dos_Descobrimentos.png/255px-S%C3%A3o_Francisco_Xavier_-_Padr%C3%A3o_dos_Descobrimentos.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a5/S%C3%A3o_Francisco_Xavier_-_Padr%C3%A3o_dos_Descobrimentos.png/340px-S%C3%A3o_Francisco_Xavier_-_Padr%C3%A3o_dos_Descobrimentos.png 2x" data-file-width="452" data-file-height="647" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:S%C3%A3o_Francisco_Xavier_-_Padr%C3%A3o_dos_Descobrimentos.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Effigy of Saint Francis Xavier in the <a href="/wiki/Padr%C3%A3o_dos_Descobrimentos" title="Padrão dos Descobrimentos">Monument to the Discoveries</a> in <a href="/wiki/Lisbon" title="Lisbon">Lisbon</a>, <a href="/wiki/Portugal" title="Portugal">Portugal</a></div></div></div>
<p>As the foremost saint from Navarre and one of the main Jesuit saints, Francis Xavier is much venerated in Spain and the Hispanic countries where <i>Francisco Javier</i> or <i>Javier</i> are common male <a href="/wiki/Given_names" class="mw-redirect" title="Given names">given names</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-INE_77-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-INE-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> The alternative spelling <i>Xavier</i> is also popular in the <a href="/wiki/Basque_Country_(greater_region)" title="Basque Country (greater region)">Basque Country</a>, <a href="/wiki/Portugal" title="Portugal">Portugal</a>, <a href="/wiki/Catalonia" title="Catalonia">Catalonia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Brazil" title="Brazil">Brazil</a>, <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">France</a>, <a href="/wiki/Belgium" title="Belgium">Belgium</a>, and southern <a href="/wiki/Italy" title="Italy">Italy</a>. In India, the spelling <i>Xavier</i> is almost always used, and the name is quite common among Christians, especially in <a href="/wiki/Goa" title="Goa">Goa</a> and in the southern states of <a href="/wiki/Tamil_Nadu" title="Tamil Nadu">Tamil Nadu</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kerala" title="Kerala">Kerala</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Karnataka" title="Karnataka">Karnataka</a>. The names <i>Francisco Xavier</i>, <i>António Xavier</i>, <i>João Xavier</i>, <i>Caetano Xavier</i>, <i>Domingos Xavier</i> and so forth, were very common till quite recently in Goa. <i>Fransiskus Xaverius</i> is commonly used as a name for <a href="/wiki/Indonesia" title="Indonesia">Indonesian</a> Catholics, usually abbreviated as FX. In Austria and <a href="/wiki/Bavaria" title="Bavaria">Bavaria</a> the name is spelt as <i>Xaver</i> (pronounced (ˈk͡saːfɐ)) and often used in addition to Francis as <i>Franz-Xaver</i> (frant͡sˈk͡saːfɐ). In <a href="/wiki/Polish_language" title="Polish language">Polish</a> the name becomes <i>Ksawery</i>. Many Catalan men are named for him, often using the two-name combination <i>Francesc Xavier</i>. In English-speaking countries, "Xavier" until recently was likely to follow "Francis"; in the 2000s, however, "Xavier" by itself became more popular than "Francis", and after 2001 featured as one of the hundred most common male baby names in the U.S.A.<sup id="cite_ref-ssa_78-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ssa-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> Furthermore, the Sevier family name, possibly most famous in the United States for <a href="/wiki/John_Sevier" title="John Sevier">John Sevier</a> (1745-1815), originated from the name "Xavier".<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Church_dedications">Church dedications</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_Xavier&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Church dedications">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Many churches all over the world, often founded by Jesuits, have been named in honour of Xavier. The many in the United States include the historic <a href="/wiki/St._Francis_Xavier_Church_(Warwick,_Maryland)" title="St. Francis Xavier Church (Warwick, Maryland)">St. Francis Xavier Shrine</a> at <a href="/wiki/Warwick,_Maryland" title="Warwick, Maryland">Warwick, Maryland</a> (founded 1720), and the <a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_St._Francis_Xavier" class="mw-redirect" title="Basilica of St. Francis Xavier">Basilica of St. Francis Xavier</a> in <a href="/wiki/Dyersville,_Iowa" title="Dyersville, Iowa">Dyersville, Iowa</a>. Note also the American educational teaching order, the <a href="/wiki/Xaverian_Brothers" title="Xaverian Brothers">Xaverian Brothers</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Mission_San_Xavier_del_Bac" title="Mission San Xavier del Bac">Mission San Xavier del Bac</a> in <a href="/wiki/Tucson,_Arizona" title="Tucson, Arizona">Tucson, Arizona</a> (founded in 1692, and known for its <a href="/wiki/Spanish_Colonial_architecture" title="Spanish Colonial architecture">Spanish Colonial architecture</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="In_art">In art</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_Xavier&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: In art">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Rubens" class="mw-redirect" title="Rubens">Rubens</a> (1577-1640) painted <i>St Francis Xavier Raising the Dead</i> for a Jesuit church in Antwerp, in which he depicted one of St Francis's many miracles.<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/Charles_Bridge" title="Charles Bridge">Charles Bridge</a> in Prague, Czech Republic, features a <a href="/wiki/Statue_of_Francis_Xavier,_Charles_Bridge" title="Statue of Francis Xavier, Charles Bridge">statue of Francis Xavier</a>.</li>
<li>In front of <a href="/wiki/Oita_Station" class="mw-redirect" title="Oita Station">Oita Station</a> of <a href="/wiki/%C5%8Cita_(city)" title="Ōita (city)">Oita City</a>, in <a href="/wiki/Oita_Prefecture" class="mw-redirect" title="Oita Prefecture">Oita Prefecture</a> (previously known as <a href="/wiki/Bungo_Province" title="Bungo Province">Bungo Province</a>) in Japan, there stands a statue of Francis Xavier.</li>
<li>The monument <a href="/wiki/Padr%C3%A3o_dos_Descobrimentos" title="Padrão dos Descobrimentos">Padrão dos Descobrimentos</a> in <a href="/wiki/Bel%C3%A9m_(Lisbon)" title="Belém (Lisbon)">Belém (Lisbon)</a>, Portugal, features a Francis Xavier image.</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Music">Music</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_Xavier&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Music">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Marc-Antoine_Charpentier" title="Marc-Antoine Charpentier">Marc-Antoine Charpentier</a>, <i>In honorem Sancti Xaverij canticum</i> H.355, for soloists, chorus, flutes, strings and continuo (1688&#160;?)</li>
<li>Marc-Antoine Charpentier, <i>Canticum de Sto Xavierio</i> H.355 a, for soloists, chorus, flutes, oboes, strings and continuo (1690).</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Missions">Missions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_Xavier&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Missions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Shortly before leaving for the East, Xavier issued a famous instruction to Father <a href="/w/index.php?title=Gaspar_Barazeuz&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Gaspar Barazeuz (page does not exist)">Gaspar Barazeuz</a> who was leaving to go to Ormuz (a kingdom on an island in the <a href="/wiki/Persian_Gulf" title="Persian Gulf">Persian Gulf</a>, formerly attached to the <a href="/wiki/Persia" class="mw-redirect" title="Persia">Empire of Persia</a>, now part of <a href="/wiki/Iran" title="Iran">Iran</a>), that he should mix with sinners:
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"/><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>And if you wish to bring forth much fruit, both for yourselves and for your neighbours, and to live consoled, converse with sinners, making them unburden themselves to you. These are the living books by which you are to study, both for your preaching and for your own consolation. I do not say that you should not on occasion read written books... to support what you say against vices with authorities from the Holy Scriptures and examples from the lives of the saints.</p><div class="templatequotecite">—&#8201;<cite><a href="#CITEREFKadič1961">Kadič 1961</a>, pp.&#160;12–18</cite></div></blockquote>
<p>Modern scholars assess the number of people converted to Christianity by Francis Xavier at around 30,000.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> While some of Xavier's methods have subsequently come under criticism (he forced converts to take <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_language" title="Portuguese language">Portuguese</a> names and to dress in Western clothes, approved the persecution of the <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox" class="mw-redirect" title="Eastern Orthodox">Eastern Church</a>, and used the <a href="/wiki/Goa" title="Goa">Goa</a> government as a missionary tool), he has also earned praise. He insisted that missionaries adapt to many of the customs, and most certainly to the language, of the culture they wish to evangelise. And unlike later missionaries, Xavier supported an educated native clergy. Though for a time it seemed that persecution had subsequently destroyed his work in <a href="/wiki/Japan" title="Japan">Japan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Protestant" class="mw-redirect" title="Protestant">Protestant</a> missionaries three centuries later discovered that approximately 100,000 Christians still practised the faith in the <a href="/wiki/Nagasaki" title="Nagasaki">Nagasaki</a> area.<sup id="cite_ref-ctlibrary_82-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ctlibrary-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Francis Xavier's work initiated permanent change in eastern <a href="/wiki/Indonesia" title="Indonesia">Indonesia</a>, and he became known as the "Apostle of the Indies" - in 1546–1547 he worked in the <a href="/wiki/Maluku_Islands" title="Maluku Islands">Maluku Islands</a> among the people of <a href="/wiki/Ambon_Island" title="Ambon Island">Ambon</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ternate" title="Ternate">Ternate</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Morotai" title="Morotai">Morotai</a> (or Moro), and laid the foundations for a permanent mission. After he left the Maluku Islands, others carried on his work, and by the 1560s there were 10,000 Roman Catholics in the area, mostly on Ambon. By the 1590s, there were 50,000 to 60,000.<sup id="cite_ref-RICKLEFSp25_83-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RICKLEFSp25-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Role_in_the_Goa_Inquisition">Role in the Goa Inquisition</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_Xavier&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Role in the Goa Inquisition">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1546, Francis Xavier proposed the establishment of the controversial <a href="/wiki/Goa_Inquisition" title="Goa Inquisition">Goa Inquisition</a> in a letter addressed to the Portuguese King, <a href="/wiki/John_III_of_Portugal" title="John III of Portugal">John III</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENeill2004160_4-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENeill2004160-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> Xavier addresses the King as the '<a href="/wiki/Vicar_of_Christ" title="Vicar of Christ">Vicar of Christ</a>', owing to his <a href="/wiki/Padroado" title="Padroado">royal patronage</a> over Christianity in the <a href="/wiki/East_Indies" title="East Indies">East Indies</a>. In a letter dated 20 January 1548, he requests the king to be tough on the Portuguese governor in India so that he may be active in propagating the faith.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENeill2004160–1_9-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENeill2004160–1-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> Xavier also wrote to the Portuguese king asking for protection in regards to new converts who were being harassed by Portuguese commandants. Francis Xavier died in 1552 without ever living to see the commencement of the Goa Inquisition.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEColeridge1872268_8-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEColeridge1872268-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="See_also">See also</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Francis_Xavier&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: See also">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<li id="cite_note-FOOTNOTENeill2004160-4"><span class="mw-cite-backlink">^ <a href="#cite_ref-FOOTNOTENeill2004160_4-0"><sup><i><b>a</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-FOOTNOTENeill2004160_4-1"><sup><i><b>b</b></i></sup></a></span> <span class="reference-text"><a href="#CITEREFNeill2004">Neill 2004</a>, p.&#160;160: "By another route I have written to your highness of the great need there is in India for preachers... The second necessity which obtains in India, if those who live there are to be good Christians, is that your highness should institute the holy Inquisition; for there are many who live according to the law of Moses or the law of Muhammad without any fear of God or shame before men".</span>

<li id="cite_note-FOOTNOTENeill2004160–1-9"><span class="mw-cite-backlink">^ <a href="#cite_ref-FOOTNOTENeill2004160–1_9-0"><sup><i><b>a</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-FOOTNOTENeill2004160–1_9-1"><sup><i><b>b</b></i></sup></a></span> <span class="reference-text"><a href="#CITEREFNeill2004">Neill 2004</a>, p.&#160;160–1:[Let the king warn the governor that] "should he fail to take active steps for the great increase of our faith, you are determined to punish him, and inform him with a solemn oath that, on his return to Portugal, all his property will be forefeited for the benefit of the Santa Misericordia, and beyond this tell him that you will keep him in irons for a number of years... There is no better way of ensuring that all in India become Christians than that your highness should inflict severe punishment on a governor".</span>
</li>
